# Round 36 of the relation-map review

Read the round-32 brief (`audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`) for scope, guarantees and the approval bar, the round-34 brief for the present name policy (`audit_evidence_claims/m34/brief.md`, as amended by round 35's brief), and the round-35 report (`audit_evidence_claims/m35/REPORT.md`). Repairs: `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` section "Round 35". Review the latest commit on HEAD. Tests: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (expect 364). Page: `python src/app.py <port>` (not 8765).

## What changed since round 35

- M35-B1: `src/app.py` `_sig` now drops marks (accents) and invisible characters before reading optical-rotation descriptors. Any bracketed group with no letters or digits that contains a sign (plus, minus sign, any dash, plus-minus), in any order, becomes "+", "\u2212" or "\u00b1" (`_SIGN_GROUP`, `_sign`); then punctuation and spaces are dropped.
- M35-S1: `src/query.py` `_named_alone` decides whether a shared-key preferred name stands alone in a question: not glued to letters or signs, and after peeling brackets that wrap it, no sign group on either side.

Report data problems separately from code problems. # Round 32 of the relation-map review

Previous rounds: `audit_evidence_claims/m1` to `m31` (`REPORT.md`; m27 and m29 stopped early, so their `attacks/` folders are the record). All repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`. Review the latest commit on HEAD. Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 361. Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>` on a port other than 8765 and exercise it over HTTP.

## What changed since round 31: a design change, not another word-list repair

Rounds 20 to 31 each found new wording that the question linker's product markers did not cover. The relation map no longer depends on those markers. See the section "Before round 32" in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, and `src/app.py` `Engine.explore`, `_sig`, `_latin`.

- **P32-1.** `explore` draws only when the whole input is one name. The linker must read exactly one entity, and its matched text must have the same letters, digits and symbols as the input (`_sig`: NFKC, casefold, any script). Case, spaces, punctuation and 짰/??짤 do not count. Anything else returns `not_one_name`, and the page asks for the name alone.
- **P32-2.** If any part of the input is refused, the whole input is refused.
- **P32-3.** A name stored for several live nodes is not drawn. The reply is `choose`: the nodes are listed and the reader clicks one, which loads it by id.
- **P32-4.** Lower-case `nct`/`chembl`/`ensg` ids are read. The whole input can be a company key or target name when `_latin(text)` equals the dictionary key, so a symbol the key would drop still blocks it (for example "Merck & Co").
- **P32-5.** This change is in the question linker, `src/query.py` `_whole_name` and the end of `_link`. A canonical key shared by several drugs is no longer decided by its spelling; only a drug's own preferred name chooses it. Inside such a key, a shorter name is not read either. Before, "medi4736" read as atezolizumab and "5-fluorouracil" as capecitabine.
- **New tests:** `tests/test_app.py::test_the_map_draws_only_a_whole_stored_name` and `test_a_key_shared_by_several_drugs_is_not_decided_by_spelling`. Older map tests that drew a centre from a longer input now go through `_page`: the map must not draw, and the question linker's reading is checked instead.
- **Docs:** README defect 15 (both languages) records that the question boxes still rely on the linker's markers for free text. Defect 9 records the shared keys. Section 0 of `web/try.html` states the one-name rule.

## Scope and approval bar

The subject is the relation map: section 0 of `web/try.html`, `POST /api/explore`, `Engine.explore`. The guarantees are those in the original brief below, with guarantee 3 now read as follows: the map draws a node only for an input that is, as a whole, one stored name of that node (or its id). It never draws a refused product, tracer or conjugate as the originator, and never draws one of several nodes that share a name.

1. Re-run every earlier reproduction from m1 to m31 against the map, and mark each finding FIXED or NOT FIXED. Reuse the earlier scripts. Inputs that were longer than a name should now be refused, `not_one_name`, `choose` or `not_found`, never a drawn originator.
2. Attack the new rule:
   - any input that draws a node the input does not name as a whole
   - a whole stored name that is in fact a different product (a data problem; say so, and separate it from code problems)
   - Unicode, symbols and invisible characters that `_sig`/`_latin` mishandle
   - the id path
   - the `choose` reply (live nodes only, injection-safe page markup, click-through)
   - company/target whole-name reads
   - retired nodes
   - anything the page text claims that the code does not do
3. Check P32-5 for regressions in the question boxes (2 to 4) and the demo examples. Report over-refusals as should-fix, not blockers.
4. Re-check guarantees A to G of the original brief briefly; earlier rounds approved them.

### Out of scope as blockers (recorded)

- The question boxes' free-text refusal relies on word lists (README defect 15). A leak there is a note, not a map blocker.
- Known data limitations, awaiting the owner's curation decision:
  - ChEMBL lists bevacizumab biosimilar development codes as bevacizumab synonyms.
  - An AACT synonym maps 13-cis-retinoic acid to CHEMBL38 tretinoin (M23-D1).
  - "Roche" is stored as a folic acid alias. The map now asks the reader to choose.
- Known over-refusals listed in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` (M5-S2, rapamycin forms, Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate, 9-amino-20(s)-camptothecin, il-6ra, TAK-659F, GS-11CAL-101, 3en3hg4wsw, an ADC named with its own payload beside it). A longer input not being drawn is the new rule, not an over-refusal.

Approval bar: no known way for the map to draw a node for an input that is not, as a whole, one stored name or id of it. That includes a refused product drawn as the originator, one of several same-named nodes drawn without the reader's choice, a wrong relation or direction, a value that differs from the stored one, and script injection. Report should-fix items separately from blockers.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m32/`. Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m32/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

# Round 34 of the relation-map review

## Before you start

- Read the round-32 brief, `audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`, for the scope, guarantees and approval bar. Read the round-33 report at `audit_evidence_claims/m33/REPORT.md`.
- Repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, section "Round 33".
- Review the latest commit on HEAD.
- Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 362.
- Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>`, on any port except 8765.

## What changed since round 33

The changes are in `src/app.py` `Engine.__init__` and `Engine.explore`, and in `src/query.py`.

- **Drugs are read only by their ChEMBL preferred name.** The map still compares names by `_sig`, as in round 33.
  - Every other stored name of a drug goes into `alias_only`: trade names, ChEMBL synonyms and registry (AACT) synonyms. Typing one returns the reply `alias_only`, and the page asks for the preferred name.
  - This replaces round 33's partial source exclusions, which had the replies `registry_name` and `biologic_synonym`.
  - Why: the round-33 data findings showed that trade names, like synonyms, file combination products, formulations and biosimilars under one molecule.
- **The map also reads:** every node's own label, target symbols, and company and disease keys. The company and disease keys only match input whose `_sig` equals the dictionary key.
- **A refused stored name stays refused, however it is spelt.** The question linker checks every stored name that matches the input's `_sig`, whether that name is read or `alias_only`. If any is refused, the input is refused. This repairs M33-B1: `Myocet??, `My/o/cet`, `Inf/lectra` and `Megace/ES` are now refused.
- **More names in `_OTHER_PRODUCT_NAMES` (`src/query.py`).** The question boxes now also refuse the round-33 combination products, plus PRED-G, NEO-DELTA-CORTEF and LEP-ETU.
- **The preferred-name rule for a shared key in a question** now needs the name to be written as a word of its own. This repairs M33-S1, "(+/-)NICOTINE".
- **Docs:** README defects 9 and 15 in both languages, and section 0 of `web/try.html`.

## What to do

1. Re-run every round-33 finding (M33-B1, D1?밆6, S1, S2) and the round-32 findings. Mark each FIXED or NOT FIXED, with exact inputs. Re-run the historical replays against the map.
2. Attack the design. Look for:
   - any input drawn for a node of which it is not, as a whole, one read name under `_sig`;
   - a read name (a ChEMBL preferred name, a node label, a target symbol, a company or disease key) that denotes a different product from the node drawn;
   - a stored name the question linker refuses that the map still draws under some spelling;
   - Unicode problems, the `choose` and `alias_only` replies and their markup, retired nodes, and the id path.
3. Check the question-linker changes for regressions in the question boxes and the demos.
4. Briefly re-check guarantees A?밎.

Report data problems separately from code problems. For each data problem give the exact input, the source record and an independent identity reference.

## Not blockers (recorded)

- Free-text leaks in the question boxes (README defect 15).
- The resolver's own brand lists in graph construction (README defect 15).
- Known over-refusals:
  - the list in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`;
  - brands and development codes that are only synonyms, which the map does not draw;
  - two tracer drugs, refused by their own labels.

## Output

- Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m34/`.
- Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m34/REPORT.md`.

# Round 35 of the relation-map review

Read the round-32 brief for the scope, guarantees and approval bar: `audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`. Read the round-34 brief and report: `audit_evidence_claims/m34/brief.md` and `audit_evidence_claims/m34/REPORT.md`. The repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, section "Round 34".

Review the latest commit on HEAD. Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 363 passing. Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>`, using any port other than 8765.

## What changed since round 34

- **M34-B1.** In `src/app.py` `Engine.__init__`, both name tables now take every stored spelling of a drug: each drug's preferred name and every entry in its alias list, plus the lookup index. The refusal check in `explore` therefore sees spellings the index dropped. One consequence: a preferred name with the same `_sig` as a refused spelling is refused too. `CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE` is refused because the resolver blocks `Cabozantinib (s)-malate`. This is recorded as an over-refusal.
- **M34-S2.** `_sig` keeps optical-rotation descriptors. `(+/-)` and `(짹)` become `짹`, `(-)` becomes `?? (U+2212), and `(+)` keeps its `+`.
- **M34-S1.** In `src/query.py`, the preferred-name rule for a shared key is blocked only by a closing bracket before the name or an opening bracket after it. Brackets around the name no longer block it.

## What to do

1. Re-run M34-B1, S1 and S2, and the round-33 and round-32 findings. Mark each FIXED or NOT FIXED, with exact inputs.
2. Re-run the historical replays against the map, including the expanded source universe: every alias in `dictionary.drugs[*].aliases`, the index, labels and keys.
3. Attack the design as in the round-34 brief.
4. Check the question-linker change for regressions.
5. Briefly re-check guarantees A to G.
6. Report data problems separately from code problems.

Not blockers, already recorded:
- Leaks in the question boxes' free text, and the resolver's own lists (README defect 15).
- The over-refusals listed in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`.
- Synonyms and brands the map does not draw.
- Two tracer drugs, and preferred names whose letters equal a refused spelling.
- THEOPHYLLINE's upstream drug type.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m35/`. Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m35/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## Code blocker

### M35-B1 ??an ignored invisible character defeats optical-sign preservation

**Location:** `src/app.py:278-282`, `_sig`. The new regular expressions recognize optical descriptors before the final character filter removes invisible characters and combining marks. An inserted U+200B is not matched by `\s*`; consequently the ASCII minus remains punctuation and is deleted by that final filter. There is no later descriptor pass.

The following are fresh HTTP results. In escaped spellings, `\u200b`, `\ufeff`, `\u2060`, and `\u0301` denote **actual single Unicode characters**, not six literal backslash characters. Complete inputs, responses, source records, and code-point captures are linked below.

| Exact input / reproducible Python spelling | `_sig` | Map result |
|---|---|---|
| `ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | center **CHEMBL269671** |
| `(-)-ARTEMISININ` | `?뭓rtemisinin` (U+2212) | `not_one_name` |
| `"(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **center CHEMBL269671** |
| `"(-\ufeff)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(-\u2060)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(-\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(\u2212\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `?뭓rtemisinin` | `not_one_name` |
| `(+)-ARTEMISININ` | `+artemisinin` | `alias_only` |
| `CHEMBL269671` | ID path | center CHEMBL269671, permitted ID control |

[Independent root HTTP/source confirmation](root_optical_confirmation.json), [separate HTTP/code-point reproduction](attacks/rotation_http.json), [70-case descriptor mutation matrix](attacks/rotation_mutations.json), [source pointers and summary](attacks/rotation_source_and_summary.json). The matrix has **56 sign-normalization invariance failures and 14 wrong-sign ARTEMISININ draws**; these are variants of one defect, not 14 independent blockers.

**Identity evidence:** `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671`, has preferred name `ARTEMISININ`, with `aliases[1].alias == "(+)-artemisinin"`; raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`, including its first ChEMBL synonym. The live node has the same preferred label. ChEBI identifies artemisinin as the positive optical form. A primary synthesis study explicitly distinguishes synthetic negative artemisinin from its natural positive antipode. [ChEBI](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316), [Krieger et al., 2018, PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/). The source record is consistent with those references; this is a **code normalization defect**, not a newly misfiled synonym.

**Why this is blocking in this round:** the new repair explicitly makes the optical descriptor part of the name, while the retained comparison contract ignores invisible characters and accents. Those rules together require `(-)-ARTEMISININ` and `(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ` to retain the same negative sign. The latter instead draws a different optical form. The exact original M34-S2 examples are fixed; that does not close this composition failure. Round 34 treated punctuation erasure as a nonblocking limitation of the then-prescribed comparison. This round explicitly changes that prescription to preserve optical descriptors. This finding therefore does not silently broaden the review to arbitrary chemical punctuation.

Under the **buggy implementation** of `_sig`, the admitted input does equal the stored signature. A purely signature-membership test consequently passes, including an independently implemented oracle that repeats the same normalization order. The counterexample rests on the two documented transformation requirements and independently established identity, rather than a claimed failure of that narrower membership test. It is not one of the recorded over-refusals, excluded brands/synonyms, tracer exceptions, or free-text question leaks.

The repair needs to make ignored-character/diacritic normalization and optical-descriptor recognition compose correctly before punctuation is discarded, or fail closed when an optical descriptor cannot be interpreted safely. Verification should preserve `??, `+`, and `짹` through invisible/combining-character variants and must not enable additional stored-name admission. No production repair was made.

All are within the HTTP length limit; maximum length is 231. The complete sweep uses direct `Engine.explore`; selected cases are separately confirmed through HTTP. [Source inventory](attacks/all_source_records.json), [independent oracle](attacks/source_audit.py), [classified counts](attacks/source_classified_summary.json).

All stored preferred-name alias/index spellings and live drug labels agree with `drug['name']`; skipping redundant preferred-name entries leaves no actual alternate-spelling gap in this dataset. [Omission closure](attacks/omitted_refusal_summary.json), [source discrepancies/equality](attacks/source_discrepancies.json).

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.
# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

+# a bracketed group of signs, dashes and slashes only: an optical-rotation descriptor
+_SIGN_GROUP = re.compile(r"[(\[][^()\[\]\w]*[+\-−±][^()\[\]\w]*[)\]]")
+
+
+def _named_alone(question: str, name: str) -> bool:
+    """The name occurs as a word of its own with no optical-rotation descriptor beside
+    it: "NICOTINE" and "(NICOTINE)", but not "(+/-)NICOTINE", "(+/-)(NICOTINE)",
+    "NICOTINE (+)" or a name glued to other letters or signs."""
+    for m in re.finditer(re.escape(name), question, re.I):
+        before, after = question[:m.start()], question[m.end():]
+        if re.search(r"[\w+\-/−±]$", before) or re.match(r"[\w+\-/−±]", after):
+            continue                              # glued to letters or signs
+        left, right = before.rstrip(), after.lstrip()
+        while left[-1:] in ("(", "[") and left and right[:1] in (")", "]") and right:
+            left, right = left[:-1].rstrip(), right[1:].lstrip()   # brackets around the name
+        if re.search(_SIGN_GROUP.pattern + r"$", left) or _SIGN_GROUP.match(right):
+            continue                              # a descriptor beside it
+        return True
+    return False
+
+

## Code blocker

### M35-B1 — an ignored invisible character defeats optical-sign preservation

**Location:** `src/app.py:278-282`, `_sig`. The new regular expressions recognize optical descriptors before the final character filter removes invisible characters and combining marks. An inserted U+200B is not matched by `\s*`; consequently the ASCII minus remains punctuation and is deleted by that final filter. There is no later descriptor pass.

The following are fresh HTTP results. In escaped spellings, `\u200b`, `\ufeff`, `\u2060`, and `\u0301` denote **actual single Unicode characters**, not six literal backslash characters. Complete inputs, responses, source records, and code-point captures are linked below.

| Exact input / reproducible Python spelling | `_sig` | Map result |
|---|---|---|
| `ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | center **CHEMBL269671** |
| `(-)-ARTEMISININ` | `−artemisinin` (U+2212) | `not_one_name` |
| `"(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **center CHEMBL269671** |
| `"(-\ufeff)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(-\u2060)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(-\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(\u2212\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name` |
| `(+)-ARTEMISININ` | `+artemisinin` | `alias_only` |
| `CHEMBL269671` | ID path | center CHEMBL269671, permitted ID control |

[Independent root HTTP/source confirmation](root_optical_confirmation.json), [separate HTTP/code-point reproduction](attacks/rotation_http.json), [70-case descriptor mutation matrix](attacks/rotation_mutations.json), [source pointers and summary](attacks/rotation_source_and_summary.json). The matrix has **56 sign-normalization invariance failures and 14 wrong-sign ARTEMISININ draws**; these are variants of one defect, not 14 independent blockers.

**Identity evidence:** `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671`, has preferred name `ARTEMISININ`, with `aliases[1].alias == "(+)-artemisinin"`; raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`, including its first ChEMBL synonym. The live node has the same preferred label. ChEBI identifies artemisinin as the positive optical form. A primary synthesis study explicitly distinguishes synthetic negative artemisinin from its natural positive antipode. [ChEBI](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316), [Krieger et al., 2018, PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/). The source record is consistent with those references; this is a **code normalization defect**, not a newly misfiled synonym.

**Why this is blocking in this round:** the new repair explicitly makes the optical descriptor part of the name, while the retained comparison contract ignores invisible characters and accents. Those rules together require `(-)-ARTEMISININ` and `(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ` to retain the same negative sign. The latter instead draws a different optical form. The exact original M34-S2 examples are fixed; that does not close this composition failure. Round 34 treated punctuation erasure as a nonblocking limitation of the then-prescribed comparison. This round explicitly changes that prescription to preserve optical descriptors. This finding therefore does not silently broaden the review to arbitrary chemical punctuation.

Under the **buggy implementation** of `_sig`, the admitted input does equal the stored signature. A purely signature-membership test consequently passes, including an independently implemented oracle that repeats the same normalization order. The counterexample rests on the two documented transformation requirements and independently established identity, rather than a claimed failure of that narrower membership test. It is not one of the recorded over-refusals, excluded brands/synonyms, tracer exceptions, or free-text question leaks.

The repair needs to make ignored-character/diacritic normalization and optical-descriptor recognition compose correctly before punctuation is discarded, or fail closed when an optical descriptor cannot be interpreted safely. Verification should preserve `−`, `+`, and `±` through invisible/combining-character variants and must not enable additional stored-name admission. No production repair was made.

## Question-linker regression, separate from the blocker

### M35-S1 — wrapping the preferred name hides an adjacent stereochemical descriptor

**Nonblocking question-only regression**, `src/query.py:898-899`. The new boundary expression permits an opening bracket immediately before the preferred-name token and a closing bracket immediately after it. It does not inspect past those brackets for a descriptor. Exact parent/current comparisons show:

| Input | Parent query.py | HEAD query.py / HTTP |
|---|---|---|
| `What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
| `What does (+/-)[NICOTINE] target?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
| `What trials evaluate [NICOTINE](+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |

The bare racemic control `(+/-)NICOTINE` names CHEMBL440464. All three question APIs receive the erroneous CHEMBL3 evidence for these wrapped cases. The map returns `not_one_name`, so this does **not** independently fail the map approval bar. [Expanded parent/current comparison](contract/expanded_question_comparison.json), [summary and HTTP cases](contract/expanded_question_summary.json), [root corroboration](root_exact_http.json).

The expanded comparison covers **six shared preferred names × 26 formats × two question wrappers = 312 cases**, with 110 changed readings (a mixture of intended recall recoveries and descriptor regressions), **zero map centers**. The inherited **99-key / 708-case** comparison and all **36 shipped demos** are unchanged. Therefore the repair note's corpus result is reproduced, but that older corpus misses the new bracket-descriptor composition. [Shared-key corpus](contract/shared_key_question_comparison.json), [demos](contract/demo_previous_comparison.json). The separate 47-product/141-question refusal matrix retains its expected refusals. [Product controls](contract/product_questions.json).

| M32-D2 `doxorubicin hydrochloride liposome` | `doxorubicinhydrochlorideliposome` → CHEMBL359744 DOXORUBICIN HYDROCHLORIDE | Liposomal formulation. [NCI](https://www.cancer.gov/about-cancer/treatment/drugs/doxorubicin-hydrochloride-liposome). |
| M32-D3 `ropeginterferon alfa-2b` | `ropeginterferonalfa2b` → CHEMBL1201558 INTERFERON ALFA-2B | PEG-conjugated interferon. [NCI](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/ropeginterferon-alfa-2b). |

All these keys are at `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.index[<key>]`; exact occurrence pointers are retained in the JSON. M33 records are trade names; M32 records are AACT synonyms. Neither the alias exclusion policy nor safe terminal replies constitute data curation. No additional admitted preferred name/node label was proved to have an erroneous source identity. That is a bounded result, not certification of all scientific identities.

## Before round 32 — the map takes one whole name (design change, not a word-list repair)

Rounds 20 to 31 each found new wording that the question linker's markers did not cover
(a phrase between the joining word and the partner, dye names outside the list). A list of
words cannot be complete, so the map no longer depends on it:

| # | Change | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P32-1 | `explore` draws only when the whole input is one name: the linker reads exactly one entity and its matched text has the same letters, digits and symbols (any script; case, spaces, punctuation, trademark signs aside) as the input. Anything more returns `not_one_name` and the page asks for the name alone | a word the linker does not know can make the name a different product; now an unknown word means "not drawn", whatever it is |
| P32-2 | any refused part refuses the whole input (before, "osimertinib, Mvasi" drew osimertinib and listed Mvasi) | one input, one name |
| P32-3 | a name stored for several nodes is not drawn; the nodes are listed and the reader picks one by id ("Roche": folic acid alias and the company) | before, the first was drawn and the others listed |
| P32-4 | lower-case ids (nct..., chembl..., ensg...) and company names the linker cuts at "&" ("Merck & Co") are read whole | recall for one-name input |
| P32-5 | the question linker no longer decides a key shared by several drugs by its spelling, and does not fall back to a shorter name inside such a key | found by a sweep of the shared keys the earlier sweeps skipped: "medi4736" was read as atezolizumab, "5-fluorouracil" as capecitabine, "ak112" as envafolimab, "peginterferon alfa-2b" as alfa-2a, "crlx101" as camptothecin, "PD-1 mAb" as pembrolizumab, "Dha paclitaxel" as paclitaxel |

The question boxes still use the linker's markers on free text; that limit is now README
defect 15. Corpus check of the question linker (9,746 intervention strings and demo
questions): 20 change, all names whose key is shared by several drugs, now unread instead of
read as one of them. Tests 361.

"""A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.

    python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765

A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
click to move on),
then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
import unicodedata
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T
from query import OncoKG

PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
PORT = 8765


class Engine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kg = OncoKG()
        # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
        # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
        self.kg.conn.close()
        self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
        self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.dic = self.kg.dic
        self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
        target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
        self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)
        # The names the relation map reads, under one rule (`_sig`). A drug is read only by
        # its ChEMBL preferred name. Every other stored name of a drug (trade names,
        # ChEMBL synonyms, synonyms copied from trial registrations) is where a product
        # gets filed under a molecule it is not: combination products under one
        # ingredient (Tobradex under dexamethasone), formulations (Myocet, LEP-ETU),
        # biosimilars under the reference molecule (Inflectra, CT-P13), and wrong
        # substances (bupivacaine hydrochloride under antimony). Those names are not read;
        # the reply asks for the preferred name. Every node's own label, target symbols,
        # and company and disease keys are read too.
        # Both tables take every stored spelling: the lookup index keeps one spelling per
        # drug and key, and a spelling it drops ("Cabozantinib (s)-malate" beside
        # "CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE") must still be checked for refusal.
        self.names: dict = {}
        self.alias_only: dict = {}
        for did, drug in self.dic["drugs"].items():
            _add_name(self.names, drug["name"], did)
            for a in drug.get("aliases") or []:
                if a.get("type") != "preferred_name":
                    _add_name(self.alias_only, a["alias"], did)
        for hits in self.dic["index"].values():
            for h in hits:
                if h.get("alias_type") != "preferred_name":
                    _add_name(self.alias_only, h["alias"], h["drug_id"])
        for r in self.kg.conn.execute("SELECT id, label FROM node"):
            _add_name(self.names, r["label"], r["id"])
        for t in self.kg.targets.values():
            _add_name(self.names, t["symbol"], t["target_id"])
        # names stored only as dictionary keys (letters and digits): company ids and
        # former names, the disease's synonyms
        self.keyed: dict = {}
        for k, ids in self.kg.companies.items():
            self.keyed.setdefault(k, []).extend(ids)
        for k in self.kg.disease["index"]:
            self.keyed.setdefault(k, []).append(self.kg.disease["disease_id"])

    def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
        out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
        for c in out["components"]:
            d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
            if d:
                c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
                             ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
                c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
                                           for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
                c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                    (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
                par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
                if par:
                    c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
                        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                        (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
            if c.get("candidates"):
                c["candidate_names"] = [
                    (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
        return out

    def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
        """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.

        `text` is a node id or one stored name, compared under one rule (`_sig`: case,
        accents, spaces, punctuation and trademark signs aside; every other letter,
        digit and symbol must match): a drug's ChEMBL preferred name, a node's own
        label, a target symbol, or a company or disease key. Anything more (a second
        name, a qualifier, a payload, a dye, a sentence) matches no stored name and is
        not drawn; the reply says to enter the name alone. A drug's other stored names
        (trade names, synonyms, registry strings) are not read: they file combination
        products, formulations, biosimilars and wrong substances under a molecule; the
        reply asks for the preferred name. A name the question linker refuses (a
        biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is refused, whether it is the input or the
        stored name the input matched. A name stored for several live nodes is not drawn
        either: they are listed and the reader picks one by id.

        Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the page states: trials by
        start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph, sponsors by trials they
        lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship keeps its role (lead
        sponsor or collaborator)."""
        conn = self.kg.conn
        matched = None
        ref = text.strip()
        if re.fullmatch(r"(?i)(nct|chembl|ensg)\d+", ref):
            ref = ref.upper()                         # an id typed in lower case
        row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                           (ref,)).fetchone()
        if row:
            nid = row["id"]
        else:
            ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
            refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
            hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
            if refused:                       # any refused part: the input is not one plain name
                return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
            sig = _sig(text)
            found = dict(self.names.get(sig, {}))
            if sig and sig == T.canonical_key(text):          # nothing the key would drop
                for k in self.keyed.get(sig, []):
                    found.setdefault(k, None)
            ids = list(found)
            choices = []
            for oid in ids:
                lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                   (oid,)).fetchone()
                if lab and all(c["id"] != oid for c in choices):
                    choices.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
            # a stored name the question linker refuses stays refused however the input
            # spells it ("Myocet" written "My/o/cet" or "Myocet\u2122")
            stored = [n for c in choices for n in found.get(c["id"]) or []]
            stored += [n for names in (self.alias_only.get(sig) or {}).values() for n in names]
            for name in stored:
                no = [e for e in self.kg.link_entities(name) if e["type"] == "Refused"]
                if no:
                    return {"refused": no[0]["label"], "why": no[0]["why"]}
            if not choices:
                if found:                     # stored, but its node is retired
                    return {"not_found": text}
                if self.alias_only.get(sig):  # a drug's trade name or synonym
                    return {"alias_only": text}
                return {"not_one_name": text} if hits else {"not_found": text}
            if len(choices) > 1:
                # one name, several nodes ("Roche"): none is drawn in place of the others;
                # the reader picks one by its id
                return {"choose": choices, "text": text}
            nid = choices[0]["id"]
            matched = (found.get(nid) or [None])[0]
        node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
                            "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
        if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
            return {"not_found": text}
        rows = conn.execute(
            "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "
            "n.props AS nprops FROM edge e JOIN node n "
            "ON n.id = CASE WHEN e.src=? THEN e.dst ELSE e.src END "
            "WHERE (e.src=? OR e.dst=?) AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND n.retired_at IS NULL",
            (nid, nid, nid)).fetchall()
        drug_ids = list({r["id"] for r in rows if r["type"] == "Drug"} | {nid})
        trials = {}
        for i in range(0, len(drug_ids), 500):
            chunk = drug_ids[i:i + 500]
            trials.update(conn.execute(               # live trials only
                "SELECT e.dst, COUNT(*) FROM edge e JOIN node t ON t.id = e.src "
                "WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND t.retired_at IS NULL "
                f"AND e.dst IN ({','.join('?' * len(chunk))}) GROUP BY e.dst", chunk).fetchall())
        groups: dict = {}
        for r in rows:
            ep, np_ = _obj(r["eprops"]), _obj(r["nprops"])
            item = {"id": r["id"], "type": r["type"], "label": r["label"], "edge_id": r["eid"]}
            role = ep.get("role") if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS" else None
            if r["type"] == "Drug":
                item["trials"] = trials.get(r["id"], 0)
                item["max_phase"] = np_.get("max_phase_overall")
            if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF":
                n = ep.get("evidence_trials")
                # a count the edge does not record stays unknown; 0 is only for sorting
                item["evidence_trials"] = n if _number(n) else None
                item["weight"] = n if _number(n) else 0
                item["sponsor_class"] = ep.get("sponsor_class")
            elif r["type"] == "Trial":
                item.update({k: np_.get(k) for k in ("phases", "status", "title", "start_date", "url")})
                item["weight"] = str(np_.get("start_date") or "")
            elif r["type"] == "Drug":
                item["weight"] = item["trials"]
            elif r["rel"] == "ASSOCIATED_WITH":
                score = ep.get("association_score")
                item["score"] = score if _number(score) else None
                item["weight"] = item["score"] or 0
            else:
                item["weight"] = 0
            if r["rel"] == "ACTS_ON":
                item["action"] = ep.get("action_type")
            if role:
                item["role"] = role
            key = (r["rel"], "out" if r["src"] == nid else "in", r["type"], role)
            groups.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
        out = []
        for (rel, direction, ntype, role), items in groups.items():
            items.sort(key=lambda x: x["label"] or "")              # names A to Z
            items.sort(key=lambda x: x["weight"], reverse=True)     # then by the stated weight
            out.append({"rel": rel, "dir": direction, "type": ntype, "role": role,
                        "total": len(items), "items": items[:per_group]})
        out.sort(key=lambda g: -g["total"])
        props = _obj(node["props"])
        return {"center": {"id": node["id"], "type": node["type"], "label": node["label"],
                           "matched_text": matched,
                           "trials": trials.get(node["id"], 0) if node["type"] == "Drug" else None,
                           "props": {k: props.get(k) for k in
                                     ("max_phase_overall", "drug_type", "phases", "status",
                                      "title", "url", "start_date") if props.get(k) is not None}},
                "groups": out}

    def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
        return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()

    def structured(self, question: str, payload: str) -> dict:
        """Check a structured answer (claims citing edges) and write the answer from it."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(question, entities), entities)
        res = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
        res["evidence"] = [{"edge_id": e.edge_id, "statement": e.statement} for e in evidence]
        return res

    def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
        """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
        checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
                                       vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
                                       linker=kg.link_entities)
        return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
                "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
                              "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
                "evidence_count": len(evidence)}


_MARKS = "®™©℠"          # registered, trade mark, copyright, service mark


def _sig(text: str) -> str:
    """The one rule by which the relation map compares a typed name with a stored one:
    trademark signs dropped before compatibility folding (so "™" never becomes the
    letters "TM"), then case and accents ignored and spaces, punctuation and invisible
    characters dropped. Letters of any script, digits and symbols are kept, so
    "trastu+zumab", "interferon" with a Greek gamma or "trastuzumab" with a radiation sign
    are not "trastuzumab" or "interferon". The sign of an optical-rotation descriptor
    is part of the name: a bracketed group of signs, dashes and slashes, in any order or
    dash style, becomes "+", "−" or "±", so "(+)-", "(-)-" and "(+/-)" stay apart
    once punctuation goes. Accents and invisible characters go first, so they cannot
    hide a sign inside such a group."""
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", "".join(c for c in text or "" if c not in _MARKS))
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", t.casefold())
    t = "".join(c for c in t if unicodedata.category(c)[0] not in "MC")
    t = _SIGN_GROUP.sub(_sign, t)
    return "".join(c for c in t if unicodedata.category(c)[0] not in "PZ")


# a bracketed group with no letters or digits: an optical-rotation descriptor or
# an empty pair of brackets
_SIGN_GROUP = re.compile(r"[(\[]([^()\[\]\w]*)[)\]]")


def _sign(m) -> str:
    g = m.group(1)
    plus = "+" in g or "±" in g
    minus = "±" in g or "−" in g or any(unicodedata.category(c) == "Pd" for c in g)
    return "±" if plus and minus else "+" if plus else "−" if minus else m.group(0)


def _add_name(into: dict, name: str, nid: str) -> None:
    if name and _sig(name):
        names = into.setdefault(_sig(name), {}).setdefault(nid, [])
        if name not in names:
            names.append(name)


def _number(v) -> bool:
    return isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) and v == v


def _obj(raw) -> dict:
    """Stored props as a dict; anything else (null, a list, broken JSON) as empty."""
    try:
        v = json.loads(raw) if raw else {}
    except ValueError:
        return {}
    return v if isinstance(v, dict) else {}


def make_handler(engine: Engine):
    class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
        def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
            self.send_response(code)
            self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
            self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(body)

        def do_GET(self):
            if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
                self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
            else:
                self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")

        def do_POST(self):
            try:
                n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
                body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
                text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()
                if len(text) > 500:                      # refused, not silently cut
                    raise ValueError("input is longer than 500 characters")
                if not text:
                    raise ValueError("empty input")
                if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check",
                                     "/api/structured", "/api/explore"):
                    return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
                with engine.lock:
                    if self.path == "/api/explore":
                        res = engine.explore(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/resolve":
                        res = engine.resolve(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/ask":
                        res = engine.ask(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/structured":
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.structured(text, answer)
                    else:
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.check(text, answer)
                self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False,
                                           allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")
            except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
                self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")

        def log_message(self, *a):
            pass
    return Handler


def main() -> None:
    port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
    engine = Engine()
    print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
    ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

# Computer Use

Use this skill to automate the UI of Microsoft Windows apps. It uses SendInput, UI Automation, and Windows.Graphics.Capture screenshots that work even when windows are occluded.

If this plugin is available, read this entire `SKILL.md` once before Windows automation work, before saying Computer Use is unavailable, and before falling back to other Windows automation.

Start with the directions in the Initialize section below. Read these bundled Markdown files relative to this `SKILL.md` when you need information about the specific topic they cover:

- `../../docs/guidance.md`: core runtime behavior, target-window workflow, screenshot handling, and recovery guidance. You MUST read this before controlling Windows apps.
- `../../docs/api.md`: full `sky` API reference. Read this when you need method signatures or object shapes.
- `../../docs/confirmations.md`: you MUST read this before deciding whether a Windows UI action needs confirmation

## Initialize

The bundled `cua_node` `@oai/sky` package is the core entry point for Computer Use. Import it directly by package name from the JavaScript session. Do not spawn `codex-computer-use.exe`, search for the helper executable, or build a custom helper protocol client.

Run this once per fresh `node_repl` JavaScript session:

```js
if (!globalThis.sky) {
  const { sky } = await import("@oai/sky");
  globalThis.sky = sky;
}
```

+import os, sys, json, subprocess, hashlib, time, pathlib, socket
+ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+OUT = ROOT / 'audit_evidence_claims/m36'
+def write(name, obj):
+    (OUT/name).write_text(json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+def hashes():
+    files = subprocess.check_output(['git','ls-files','-z'], cwd=ROOT).decode().split('\0')
+    files = sorted(set(x for x in files if x) | {'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite','data/processed/resolved.json'})
+    return {p:hashlib.sha256((ROOT/p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files if (ROOT/p).is_file()}
+if __name__ == '__main__':
+    mode = sys.argv[1]
+    if mode == 'before':
+        write('integrity_before.json', {'head':subprocess.check_output(['git','rev-parse','HEAD'],cwd=ROOT).decode().strip(),'hashes':hashes()})
+        print('baseline saved', flush=True)
+    elif mode == 'tests':
+        temp=OUT/'tmp'; temp.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
+        env=dict(os.environ, PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1', PYTHONIOENCODING='utf-8', TEMP=str(temp), TMP=str(temp), TMPDIR=str(temp))
+        start=time.perf_counter()
+        with (OUT/'pytest.txt').open('w',encoding='utf-8') as f:
+            p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-m','pytest','tests','-q','-p','no:cacheprovider'],cwd=ROOT,env=env,stdout=f,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
+        meta={'command':'python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider','exit_code':p.returncode,'elapsed_seconds':time.perf_counter()-start}
+        write('pytest_meta.json',meta); print(json.dumps(meta),flush=True)
+    elif mode == 'after':

+import ast, json, pathlib, re, subprocess, sys, unicodedata as U, urllib.request
+ROOT=pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+OUT=ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m36'
+sys.path.insert(0,str(ROOT/'src'))
+import app
+def save(name,obj): (OUT/name).write_text(json.dumps(obj,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
+parent=subprocess.check_output(['git','show','HEAD^:src/app.py'],cwd=ROOT).decode()
+tree=ast.parse(parent)
+node=next(x for x in tree.body if isinstance(x,ast.FunctionDef) and x.name=='_sig')
+namespace={'re':re,'unicodedata':U,'_MARKS':app._MARKS}
+exec(compile(ast.Module(body=[node],type_ignores=[]),'HEAD^:src/app.py','exec'),namespace)
+old_sig=namespace['_sig']
+def stages(t):
+    n=U.normalize('NFKC',''.join(c for c in t if c not in app._MARKS))
+    d=U.normalize('NFKD',n.casefold())
+    stripped=''.join(c for c in d if U.category(c)[0] not in 'MC')
+    sign=app._SIGN_GROUP.sub(app._sign,stripped)
+    return {'input':t,'nfkc':n,'nfkd_casefold':d,'no_marks_controls':stripped,'descriptor_matches':[m.group(0) for m in app._SIGN_GROUP.finditer(stripped)],'after_descriptors':sign,'result':app._sig(t),'parent_sig':old_sig(t)}
+cases=['(-)-ARTEMISININ','(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ','(-_)-ARTEMISININ','(-\u200b_\u0301)-ARTEMISININ','(-\uff3f)-ARTEMISININ','(-())-ARTEMISININ','(+/-)NICOTINE','(+/-\u2622)NICOTINE','(+/-\u00d7)NICOTINE','(+/-)NICOTINE\u2622']
+save('normalization_stages.json',[stages(t) for t in cases])
+eligible=[]; failures=[]
+for i in range(0x110000):
+    c=chr(i)
+    if U.category(c)[0] not in 'PZMC' or c in '()[]': continue
+    folded=U.normalize('NFKD',U.normalize('NFKC',c).casefold())
+    folded=''.join(x for x in folded if U.category(x)[0] not in 'MC')
+    # Scope: ignored characters whose normalized form has no letter, digit,
+    # additional sign, or grouping delimiter. This predicate is independent of regex \w.
+    if any(U.category(x)[0] not in 'PZ' or x in '()[]' or U.category(x)=='Pd' for x in folded): continue
+    t='(-'+c+')-ARTEMISININ'
+    row={'codepoint':'U+%04X'%i,'name':U.name(c,''),'category':U.category(c),'input_escaped':t.encode('unicode_escape').decode(),'normalized_insert':folded,'sig':app._sig(t)}
+    eligible.append(row)
+    if row['sig']!='−artemisinin': failures.append(row)
+save('ignored_character_matrix.json',{'eligible_count':len(eligible),'failure_count':len(failures),'failures':failures,'predicate':'P/Z/M/C, normalized to punctuation/spacing/empty without sign or grouping delimiter'})
+rows=[]
+for r in failures:
+    t=r['input_escaped'].encode().decode('unicode_escape')
+    req=urllib.request.Request('http://127.0.0.1:8936/api/explore',data=json.dumps({'text':t}).encode(),headers={'Content-Type':'application/json'})
+    with urllib.request.urlopen(req,timeout=180) as resp: result=json.load(resp)
+    rows.append(dict(r,response=result))
+save('ignored_character_http.json',rows)
+dic=json.loads((ROOT/'data/processed/resolved.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))['dictionary']
+raw=json.loads((ROOT/'data/raw/drugs.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
+source=[]
+for did in ['CHEMBL269671','CHEMBL440464','CHEMBL1355736']:
+    record=dic['drugs'][did]
+    raw_hits=[{'json_path':f'data[{i}].drug','record':r['drug']} for i,r in enumerate(raw['data']) if r.get('drug',{}).get('id')==did]
+    source.append({'drug_id':did,'dictionary_path':f'dictionary.drugs.{did}','dictionary_record':record,'raw_records':raw_hits})
+save('focused_source_records.json',source)
+print(json.dumps({'eligible_count':len(eligible),'failures':failures,'http_wrong_draws':sum('center' in r['response'] for r in rows)},ensure_ascii=False))

from pathlib import Path
sys.dont_write_bytecode = True

from app import Engine
import textrules as T

P = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
def write(name, value):
    (P / name).write_text(json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')

def sig(s):
    # Independent character-by-character oracle for the amended name contract.
    # A descriptor has no letters/digits; underscore is punctuation, not a letter.
    s = unicodedata.normalize('NFKC', ''.join(c for c in s if c not in '\u00ae\u2122\u00a9\u2120'))
    s = unicodedata.normalize('NFKD', s.casefold())
    s = ''.join(c for c in s if unicodedata.category(c)[0] not in 'MC')
    out=[]; pos=0
    while pos<len(s):
        if s[pos] in '([':
            stop=pos+1
            while stop<len(s) and s[stop] not in '()[]':stop+=1
            if stop<len(s) and s[stop] in ')]':
                inside=s[pos+1:stop]
                if not any(c.isalnum() for c in inside):
                    plus=any(c in '+?' for c in inside)
                    minus=any(c in '??' or unicodedata.category(c)=='Pd' for c in inside)
                    if plus or minus:
                        out.append('?' if plus and minus else '+' if plus else '?')
                        pos=stop+1;continue
        out.append(s[pos]);pos+=1
    return ''.join(c for c in out if unicodedata.category(c)[0] not in 'PZ')

allowed=collections.defaultdict(set); keyed=collections.defaultdict(set); stored=collections.defaultdict(set)
alltexts=set();sources=[]
def add(t,n,read,source,kind='name'):
    if not t or not sig(t):return
    alltexts.add(t)
    if kind!='index_key':stored[sig(t)].add(t)
    sources.append({'text':t,'id':n,'read':read,'kind':kind,'source':source})
    if read:(keyed if kind=='key' else allowed)[sig(t)].add(n)
for did,d in e.dic['drugs'].items():
    add(d['name'],did,True,{'pointer':f'dictionary.drugs.{did}.name'})
    for i,a in enumerate(d.get('aliases') or []):
        add(a['alias'],did,a.get('type')=='preferred_name',{'pointer':f'dictionary.drugs.{did}.aliases[{i}]',**a})
for k,hits in e.dic['index'].items():
    for h in hits:
        add(h['alias'],h['drug_id'],h.get('alias_type')=='preferred_name',{'dictionary_key':k,**h})
        add(k,h['drug_id'],False,{'dictionary_index_key':k},kind='index_key')
for r in e.kg.conn.execute('SELECT id,type,label FROM node'):
    add(r['label'],r['id'],True,dict(r))
for t in e.kg.targets.values():add(t['symbol'],t['target_id'],True,{'target':t})
for k,ids in e.kg.companies.items():
    for nid in ids:add(k,nid,True,{'company_key':k},kind='key')
for k in e.kg.disease['index']:add(k,e.kg.disease['disease_id'],True,{'disease_key':k},kind='key')
write('all_source_records.json',sources)
label_mismatches=[{'node':r,'drug_name':e.dic['drugs'][r['id']]['name']} for r in live.values() if r['id'] in e.dic['drugs'] and r['label']!=e.dic['drugs'][r['id']]['name']]
preferred_mismatches=[r for r in sources if r['read'] and ('alias' in r['source']) and r['text']!=e.dic['drugs'].get(r['id'],{}).get('name')]
indexed={(h['drug_id'],h['alias']) for hs in e.dic['index'].values() for h in hs}
omitted=[{'id':did,'preferred':d['name'],'alias_record':a} for did,d in e.dic['drugs'].items() for a in d.get('aliases',[]) if (did,a['alias']) not in indexed]
write('source_discrepancies.json',{'label_mismatches':label_mismatches,'preferred_mismatches':preferred_mismatches,'unindexed_alias_spellings':omitted})
print('source names',len(alltexts),'records',len(sources),'omitted',len(omitted),flush=True)
start=time.monotonic();refused={};linker={};errors=[]
for i,t in enumerate(sorted(alltexts)):
    try:
        ents=e.kg.link_entities(t);linker[t]=ents
        no=[x for x in ents if x['type']=='Refused']
        if no:refused[t]=no
    except Exception as ex:errors.append({'phase':'linker','text':t,'error':repr(ex)})
    if i%2000==0:print('linker',i,'refused',len(refused),'seconds',round(time.monotonic()-start,1),flush=True)
write('refused_source_names.json',refused)
refused_sigs={sig(t) for t in refused if t in stored[sig(t)]}
results=[];failures=[];counts=collections.Counter()
for i,t in enumerate(sorted(alltexts)):
    try:r=compact(e.explore(t))
    except Exception as ex:errors.append({'phase':'map','text':t,'error':repr(ex)});continue
    kind=next(iter(r));counts[kind]+=1
    ids=allowed[sig(t)]&live.keys()
    if sig(t)==T.canonical_key(t):ids|=keyed[sig(t)]&live.keys()
    isid=t.strip() in live or t.strip().upper() in live and bool(re.fullmatch(r'(?i)(nct|chembl|ensg)\d+',t.strip()))
    row={'text':t,'response':r,'expected_live_ids':sorted(ids)};results.append(row)
    if 'center' in r:
        if not isid and r['center']['id'] not in ids:failures.append({'kind':'membership',**row})
        if not isid and len(ids)>1:failures.append({'kind':'silent_ambiguity',**row})
        if not isid and sig(t) in refused_sigs:failures.append({'kind':'refused_stored_signature',**row})
    if not isid and sig(t) in refused_sigs and 'refused' not in r:failures.append({'kind':'refusal_reply',**row})
    if 'choose' in r and {x['id'] for x in r['choose']}!=ids:failures.append({'kind':'choices',**row})
    if i%2000==0:print('map',i,dict(counts),'seconds',round(time.monotonic()-start,1),flush=True)
write('source_sweep.json',results)
summary={'source_names':len(alltexts),'source_records':len(sources),'drug_alias_records':sum(len(d.get('aliases',[])) for d in e.dic['drugs'].values()),'live_nodes':len(live),'response_counts':counts,'refused_stored_names':len(refused),'refused_signatures':len(refused_sigs),'failures':failures,'errors':errors,'seconds':time.monotonic()-start}
write('source_summary.json',summary)

+def stages(t):

+    sign=app._SIGN_GROUP.sub(app._sign,stripped)

+    # additional sign, or grouping delimiter. This predicate is independent of regex \w.

finally:

+# Independent relation-map review — round 36
+
+Reviewed **HEAD `c3040c184ad4da0fc638d61ab94d46501c61a11a`**, parent `56eb3d6297f2878d008e5edd9eb5eade62a0841c`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED: two normalization blockers.** The exact M35-B1 and M35-S1 reproductions are fixed. A punctuation character can still hide a negative optical descriptor, and the new descriptor substitution deletes unrelated significant symbols. A separate nested-descriptor question regression is nonblocking under the specified scope. No new upstream data defect was established.
+
+Scope is section 0 of `web/try.html`, `POST /api/explore`, and `Engine.explore`, under the [round-32 approval bar](../m32/brief.md), [round-34 name policy](../m34/brief.md), its [round-35 amendment](../m35/brief.md), and [round-35 report](../m35/REPORT.md). FIXED below closes an exact reproduction under the current preferred-name policy; it does not certify all related spellings, restore excluded aliases, or imply source-data curation. This is a review; no production repair was made.
+
+## Verification
+
+- **`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`: 364 passed in 129.57 seconds**, exit 0; wrapper elapsed 132.828 seconds. One warning came from a subprocess reader decoding UTF-8 output as CP949 in `test_correction_check_fails_when_a_correction_is_pending`. That test was rerun with `PYTHONUTF8=1`: **1 passed in 3.94 seconds**, no warning. The full-suite warning is retained rather than described as a warning-free run. [Full output](pytest.txt), [command/timing](pytest_meta.json), [focused recheck](pytest_warning_recheck.txt).
+- Started the actual CLI using **`python src/app.py 8936`**. Root retained **274 HTTP response records**: 124 baseline, 85 exact/adversarial, 36 supplement, 22 new attack cases, and seven Unicode confirmations. This counts retained records, not every request made by all lanes. Empty input and 501-character input produce the two expected baseline HTTP 400 responses. The served page is byte-identical to `web/try.html`. [Baseline](root_confirmation.json), [bodies](root_http.json), [exact cases](root_exact_http.json), [supplement](root_supplement_http.json), [new attacks](root_new_attack_http.json), [Unicode HTTP](ignored_character_http.json).
+- **4,689 distinct historical/current inputs**: 301 centers, 1,434 refusals, 2,555 `alias_only`, 327 `not_one_name`, 68 `not_found`, four choices; zero exceptions, independent admitted-name membership failures, silent ambiguous draws, or wrong choice sets. Four changes against the 4,673 prior corpus inputs are intended mark-removal recall: `"trastuzumab\u034f"`, `"trastuzumab\ufe0f"`, `"trastuzumab\U000e0100"`, and `"trastu\u034fzumab"` now draw CHEMBL1201585. Actual Unicode characters are meant in these escaped spellings. [Corpus summary](replay/corpus_summary.json), [responses](replay/corpus_results.json), [parent differences](replay/parent_corpus_changes.json).
+
+## Code blockers
+
+### M36-B1 — underscore punctuation still erases a negative optical descriptor
+
+**Location:** `src/app.py:282-296`, especially `_SIGN_GROUP` at line 289 and the final punctuation filter at line 284. The comment and amended contract say a group with no letters or digits is recognized. Python `\w` also includes `_`, however. `(-_)` therefore does not match; the subsequent punctuation filter deletes both the hyphen and underscore. The sign is never recovered.
+
+| Exact input / Python spelling | Current `_sig` | Fresh HTTP result |
+|---|---|---|
+| `ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | center **CHEMBL269671** |
+| `(-)-ARTEMISININ` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name` |
+| `"(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name`; M35-B1 exact repair works |
+| `(-_)-ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | **center CHEMBL269671** |
+| `(_-)-ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `[-_]-ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\u200b_\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\uff3f)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center**; fullwidth underscore folds to `_` |
+| `"(-\u203f)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name`; undertie punctuation control |
+| `CHEMBL269671` | permitted ID path | center CHEMBL269671 |
+
+This is a residual composition defect, present in the parent too, not a claim that the four original M35 inputs still fail. Removing marks/invisibles first correctly repairs those inputs; it does not repair a punctuation character that the descriptor recognizer mistakenly classifies as a word character. [Exact HTTP/code points](root_new_attack_http.json), [step-by-step normalization and parent signatures](normalization_stages.json), [full parent/current comparison](root_parent_comparison.json).
+
+An independent enumeration of **3,501 assigned punctuation/spacing/mark/control characters** whose normalized form is ignorable and contains no additional sign, letter, digit, or grouping delimiter finds **seven failures**: U+005F, U+FE33, U+FE34, U+FE4D, U+FE4E, U+FE4F, U+FF3F. All seven normalize to underscore and all seven draw CHEMBL269671 over HTTP. Unassigned code points, private-use characters, and surrogates are excluded from this count. [Predicate, failures](ignored_character_matrix.json), [HTTP confirmations](ignored_character_http.json), [reproducible checker](root_composition.py).
+
+**Identity evidence:** `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671.name` is `ARTEMISININ`, with `aliases[1].alias == "(+)-artemisinin"`; raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`. ChEBI identifies the natural compound as the positive optical form; primary synthesis research explicitly distinguishes negative artemisinin from its natural positive antipode. These references were reopened during this review. [Source records](focused_source_records.json), [ChEBI](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316), [Krieger et al., PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/).
+
+Thus the source assignment is consistent; the wrong draw is caused by code dropping the input's negative descriptor. The same issue appears with nested empty punctuation, e.g. `(-())-ARTEMISININ`, but the blocker requires only the unambiguous underscore examples above.
+
+### M36-B2 — descriptor substitution consumes unrelated significant symbols
+
+**Location:** `src/app.py:289-296`. The broad pattern accepts any non-word, non-bracket character; `_sign` replaces the entire match with one sign and discards every other symbol it contained. This newly weakens the retained rule that letters, digits and symbols must match (`Engine.explore`, lines 110–115; `_sig`, lines 270–275).
+
+| Exact input | Parent result | HEAD signature / fresh result |
+|---|---|---|
+| `(+/-)NICOTINE` | CHEMBL440464 | `±nicotine`; same legitimate center |
+| `(+/-☢)NICOTINE` | `not_found` | `±nicotine`; **CHEMBL440464** |
+| `(±☢)NICOTINE` | `not_found` | `±nicotine`; **CHEMBL440464** |
+| `(+/-$)NICOTINE` | `not_found` | `±nicotine`; **CHEMBL440464** |
+| `(+/-×)NICOTINE` | `not_found` | `±nicotine`; **CHEMBL440464** |
+| `(+/-)NICOTINE☢` | `not_found` | `±nicotine☢`; `not_found` |
+
+The stored preferred name is `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL440464.name == "(+/-)NICOTINE"`. It does not contain ☢ (U+2622, `So`), $ (U+0024, `Sc`), or × (U+00D7, `Sm`). These are neither punctuation nor the ignored trademark marks nor rotation signs. The parent/current comparison loads **both parent `app.py` and parent `query.py`**, so this is a demonstrated commit regression rather than only a theoretical regex difference. Root HTTP confirms the current draws. [Parent/current responses](root_parent_comparison.json), [HTTP](root_new_attack_http.json), [normalization stages](normalization_stages.json), [source record](focused_source_records.json).
+
+The round-36 change summary accurately describes the broad collapse of a sign-containing group with no letters/digits. It does **not** remove the retained approval bar or the explicit guarantee that other significant symbols match. This finding identifies that design conflict: broad group collapse cannot be used to justify dropping arbitrary symbols. No assertion that `☢` alone identifies a particular isotope or pharmaceutical product is needed; the input contains a significant symbol absent from the admitted name.
+
+Across the source inventory, **14 distinct optical-descriptor spellings / 30 source occurrences × six extra-symbol mutations = 84 cases** all lose the extra symbol. Six variants of the admitted `(+/-)NICOTINE` preferred name draw CHEMBL440464. Other source spellings are aliases and stay excluded. [Descriptor source inventory](attacks/descriptor_source_records.json), [all mutations](attacks/sign_symbol_composition.json), [summary](attacks/sign_symbol_composition_summary.json).
+
+## Question-linker regression — nonblocking
+
+### M36-S1 — an extra pair of brackets around the descriptor hides it
+
+**Location:** `src/query.py:118-134`, `_named_alone`. It peels brackets surrounding the name, then requires the adjacent descriptor to match immediately at the edge of the remaining text. It does not look through an extra pair of brackets around that descriptor.
+
+| Exact question | Parent linker | HEAD / question APIs |
+|---|---|---|
+| `What trials evaluate ((+/-))NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | **CHEMBL3 NICOTINE**, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate NICOTINE((+/-))?` | no entity/evidence | **CHEMBL3 NICOTINE**, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?` | CHEMBL3 | no entity/evidence; original M35-S1 fixed |
+| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)?` | CHEMBL3 | CHEMBL3; ordinary bracket recall retained |
+
+The incorrect nested-descriptor reading reaches `/api/ask`, `/api/structured`, and `/api/check`; the map returns `not_one_name`. Therefore it is **not a map blocker**, consistent with README defect 15 and the review scope. [36 HTTP records and parent controls](contract/question_regression_http.json), [540-case composition matrix](contract/question_composition.json), [summary](contract/question_composition_summary.json).
+
+The new matrix covers six shared preferred names × 45 formats × two question wrappers: **540 cases, 130 changed readings, zero map centers**. Changes include successful M35 repair cases as well as the new regression; 130 is not a defect count. All **36 shipped demos** and the inherited **99-key / 708-case** shared-name corpus remain parent-identical. [Demos](contract/demo_previous_comparison.json), [shared-key comparison](contract/shared_key_question_comparison.json).

+import os, sys, json, subprocess, hashlib, time, pathlib, socket
+ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+OUT = ROOT / 'audit_evidence_claims/m36'
+def write(name, obj):
+    (OUT/name).write_text(json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+def hashes():
+    files = subprocess.check_output(['git','ls-files','-z'], cwd=ROOT).decode().split('\0')
